# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Calculates the total revenue from all rental records in the store by summing their totalPrice values.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each rental in the rentals list:
     - Add rental.totalPrice to total.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: rentals (List<Rental>)
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: 
  - double: the sum of totalPrice across all rentals. Returns 0.0 if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: 
  - The store's state remains unchanged.
  - Returns the sum of all rental.totalPrice values.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Returns all cars that are currently available (not in an active rental with no return date), sorted by daily price in ascending order.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty list availableCars.
  2. For each car in the cars list:
     - Check if the car is NOT associated with any rental that has backDate == null.
       (i.e., no active rental for that car).
     - If no such active rental exists, add the car to availableCars.
  3. Sort availableCars by dailyPrice in ascending order.
  4. Return availableCars.
Related Features: cars (List<Car>), rentals (List<Rental>)
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: 
  - List<Car>: list of available cars sorted by daily price ascending. Empty list if none available.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: 
  - The store's state remains unchanged.
  - Returns a sorted list of cars not currently rented out (no active rental with null backDate).

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Identifies customers whose rentals are overdue (return date empty and current date past due date).
Algorithm:
  1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
  2. Initialize an empty list overdueCustomers.
  3. For each rental in the rentals list:
     - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
       - Add rental.customer to overdueCustomers if not already present.
  4. Return overdueCustomers.
Related Features: rentals (List<Rental>)
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input: 
  - currentDate: EDate - the date to compare against due dates. Can be null.
Output: 
  - List<Customer>: list of customers with overdue rentals. Empty list if none or if currentDate is null.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: 
  - The store's state remains unchanged.
  - Returns a list of unique customers whose rentals meet the overdue criteria.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts how many rental records each customer has and returns the mapping.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty map (EMap) result.
  2. For each rental in the rentals list:
     - Get the customer from the rental.
     - Increment the count for that customer in the map (default 0 if not present).
  3. Return the result map.
Related Features: rentals (List<Rental>)
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: 
  - EMap: a mapping from Customer to Integer (count of rentals). Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: 
  - The store's state remains unchanged.
  - Returns a map with accurate rental counts per customer.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Computes the average daily price of all cars in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. If cars list is empty, return 0.0.
  2. Initialize total = 0.0.
  3. For each car in the cars list:
     - Add car.dailyPrice to total.
  4. Return total / size of cars list.
Related Features: cars (List<Car>)
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: 
  - double: the arithmetic mean of all car daily prices. Returns 0.0 if no cars exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: 
  - The store's state remains unchanged.
  - Returns the average daily price, or 0.0 if the car list is empty.

```result
/*
 * revised description for operation_1
 */

class_1::operation_2
/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...
```

# Operation Specifications
OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Sends an overdue notice to the specified customer by generating a formatted notice string.
Algorithm:
  1. Retrieve the customer's full name (name + " " + surname) and address.
  2. Format a notice string containing the noticeId, customer details, and a standard overdue message.
  3. Return the formatted notice string.
Related Features: noticeId, customer (Customer object)
Related Classes: Customer
Input: 
  - customer: Customer - the target customer to receive the notice, must not be null.
Output: 
  - String: a formatted notice message containing noticeId, customer name, address, and overdue alert text.
Pre-condition: 
  - The OverdueNotice object has a non-null noticeId.
  - The provided customer parameter is not null and has valid name, surname, and address.
Post-condition: 
  - A formatted notice string is returned. No system state is modified.

# Operation Specifications
OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Sends an overdue notice to the specified customer by generating a formatted notice string.
Algorithm:
  1. Retrieve the customer's full name (name + " " + surname) and address.
  2. Format a notice string containing the noticeId, customer details, and a standard overdue message.
  3. Return the formatted notice string.
Related Features: noticeId, customer (Customer object)
Related Classes: Customer
Input: 
  - customer: Customer - the target customer to receive the notice, must not be null.
Output: 
  - String: a formatted notice message containing noticeId, customer name, address, and overdue alert text.
Pre-condition: 
  - The OverdueNotice object has a non-null noticeId.
  - The provided customer parameter is not null and has valid name, surname, and address.
Post-condition: 
  - A formatted notice string is returned. No system state is modified.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Returns all cars that are currently available (not in an active rental with no return date), sorted by daily price in ascending order.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize an empty list availableCars.
   *   2. For each car in the cars list:
   *      - Check if the car is NOT associated with any rental that has backDate == null.
   *        (i.e., no active rental for that car).
   *      - If no such active rental exists, add the car to availableCars.
   *   3. Sort availableCars by dailyPrice in ascending order.
   *   4. Return availableCars.
   * Related Features: cars (List<Car>), rentals (List<Rental>)
   * Related Classes: Car, Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: 
   *   - List<Car>: list of available cars sorted by daily price ascending. Empty list if none available.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: 
   *   - The store's state remains unchanged.
   *   - Returns a sorted list of cars not currently rented out (no active rental with null backDate).
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean hasActiveRental = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          hasActiveRental = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!hasActiveRental) {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    // Sort by dailyPrice in ascending order
    availableCars.sort(new java.util.Comparator<Car>() {
      @Override
      public int compare(Car c1, Car c2) {
        return Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice());
      }
    });
    return availableCars;
  }

   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalRevenue()
  {
    double total = 0.0;
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      total += rental.getTotalPrice();
    }
    return total;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Identifies customers whose rentals are overdue (return date empty and current date past due date).
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
   *   2. Initialize an empty list overdueCustomers.
   *   3. For each rental in the rentals list:
   *      - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
   *        - Add rental.customer to overdueCustomers if not already present.
   *   4. Return overdueCustomers.
   * Related Features: rentals (List<Rental>)
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
   * Input: 
   *   - currentDate: EDate - the date to compare against due dates. Can be null.
   * Output:

   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: 
   *   - The store's state remains unchanged.
   *   - Returns a list of unique customers whose rentals meet the overdue criteria.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!overdueCustomers.contains(customer)) {
          overdueCustomers.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return overdueCustomers;
  }

   *   - The store's state remains unchanged.
   *   - Returns the average daily price, or 0.0 if the car list is empty.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double determineAverageDailyPrice()
  {
    if (getCars().isEmpty()) {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double total = 0.0;
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      total += car.getDailyPrice();
    }
    return total / getCars().size();
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Counts how many rental records each customer has and returns the mapping.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize an empty map (EMap) result.
   *   2. For each rental in the rentals list:
   *      - Get the customer from the rental.
   *      - Increment the count for that customer in the map (default 0 if not present).
   *   3. Return the result map.
   * Related Features: rentals (List<Rental>)
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
   * Input: None
   * Output: 
   *   - EMap: a mapping from Customer to Integer (count of rentals). Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: 
   *   - The store's state remains unchanged.

   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {
    Map<Customer, Integer> result = new java.util.HashMap<Customer, Integer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
      Integer count = result.get(customer);
      if (count == null) {
        result.put(customer, 1);
      } else {
        result.put(customer, count + 1);
      }
    }
    return result;
  }

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {

    for (Car car : getCars()) {

      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          hasActiveRental = true;
          break;

      }
    });
    return availableCars;

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!overdueCustomers.contains(customer)) {

```java
   *   1. Retrieve the customer's full name (name + " " + surname) and address.
```

   *   - customer: Customer - the target customer to receive the notice, must not be null.
   * Output: 
   *   - String: a formatted notice message containing noticeId, customer name, address, and overdue alert text.
   * Pre-condition: 
   *   - The OverdueNotice object has a non-null noticeId.
   *   - The provided customer parameter is not null and has valid name, surname, and address.
   * Post-condition: 
   *   - A formatted notice string is returned. No system state is modified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String sendNoticeTo(final Customer customer)
  {
    String fullName = customer.getName() + " " + customer.getSurname();
    String address = customer.getAddress();
    String notice = "Overdue Notice #" + getNoticeId() + "\n"
        + "Customer: " + fullName + "\n"
        + "Address: " + address + "\n"
        + "This is a notice that your rental period has passed the due date. "
        + "Please return the vehicle as soon as possible to avoid additional fees.";
    return notice;
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operations
+ OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
+ Store::calculateTotalRevenue
+ Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
+ Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
+ Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
+ Store::identifyAvailableCars

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Finds and returns a list of customers whose rentals are overdue (return date empty and current date past due date).
Algorithm:
  1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
  2. Create an empty list of customers (result list).
  3. For each rental in rentals:
       - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
           - Add rental.customer to result list (if not already present, to avoid duplicates).
  4. Return result list.
Related Features: rentals (attribute)
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input: currentDate : EDate – the date to compare against due dates. Can be null.
Output: List<Customer> – list of customers with overdue rentals. Returns empty list if currentDate is null or no overdue rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains each customer who has at least one rental where backDate is null and currentDate > dueDate. No duplicate customers appear.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identifies and returns all cars currently available for rent, sorted by daily price in ascending order.
Algorithm:
  1. Create a list to hold available cars.
  2. For each car in cars:
       - Check if there exists any rental in rentals such that:
           rental.car == car AND rental.backDate is null.
       - If no such rental exists, add car to available list.
  3. Sort the available list by dailyPrice in ascending order.
  4. Return the sorted list.
Related Features: cars (attribute), rentals (attribute)
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> – list of available cars sorted by dailyPrice ascending. Returns empty list if no cars are available or store has no cars.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains all cars from the cars list that are not currently rented (i.e., no active rental with null backDate). The list is sorted by dailyPrice in ascending order.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts how many rental records each customer has and returns the result as a mapping.
Algorithm:
  1. Create an empty map (EMap) where keys are Customer objects and values are integers.
  2. If rentals list is empty, return the empty map.
  3. For each rental in rentals:
       - Let c = rental.customer
       - If c is already a key in the map, increment its count by 1.
       - Otherwise, add c with count = 1.
  4. Return the map.
Related Features: rentals (attribute)
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap – a map from Customer to Integer, representing the number of rentals per customer. Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned map contains exactly one entry per distinct customer found in the rentals list, with the count equal to the number of rentals associated with that customer.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Calculates and returns the total revenue from all rental records in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each rental in the rentals list:
       - Add rental.totalPrice to total.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: rentals (attribute)
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double – the sum of totalPrice of all rentals in the store. Returns 0.0 if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned value equals the sum of totalPrice for all Rental objects in the rentals list.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Computes and returns the average daily price of all cars in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. If cars list is empty, return 0.0.
  2. Initialize sum = 0.0.
  3. For each car in cars:
       - Add car.dailyPrice to sum.
  4. Return sum / cars.size().
Related Features: cars (attribute)
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double – the arithmetic mean of dailyPrice of all cars in the store. Returns 0.0 if no cars exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned value is either 0.0 (if cars list is empty) or the sum of all car dailyPrices divided by the number of cars.

# Operation Specifications
Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Finds and returns a list of customers whose rentals are overdue (return date empty and current date past due date).
Algorithm:
  1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.

  3. For each rental in rentals:
       - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
           - Add rental.customer to result list (if not already present, to avoid duplicates).
  4. Return result list.

Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input: currentDate : EDate – the date to compare against due dates. Can be null.

Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains each customer who has at least one rental where backDate is null and currentDate > dueDate. No duplicate customers appear.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identifies and returns all cars currently available for rent, sorted by daily price in ascending order.
Algorithm:

  2. For each car in cars:
       - Check if there exists any rental in rentals such that:
           rental.car == car AND rental.backDate is null.
       - If no such rental exists, add car to available list.
  3. Sort the available list by dailyPrice in ascending order.
  4. Return the sorted list.
Related Features: cars (attribute), rentals (attribute)
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> – list of available cars sorted by dailyPrice ascending. Returns empty list if no cars are available or store has no cars.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains all cars from the cars list that are not currently rented (i.e., no active rental with null backDate). The list is sorted by dailyPrice in ascending order.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts how many rental records each customer has and returns the result as a mapping.

  3. For each rental in rentals:
       - Let c = rental.customer
       - If c is already a key in the map, increment its count by 1.
       - Otherwise, add c with count = 1.
  4. Return the map.
Related Features: rentals (attribute)
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap – a map from Customer to Integer, representing the number of rentals per customer. Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None

Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
```text
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Sends an overdue notice to the specified customer and returns a confirmation message.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that the customer parameter is not null.
  2. Generate a unique noticeId (e.g., timestamp-based or UUID).
  3. Set the customer field of this OverdueNotice to the provided customer.
  4. Simulate sending the notice (e.g., print/log the notice details).
  5. Return a string confirming the notice has been sent, including the noticeId.
Related Features: noticeId, customer (attribute)
Related Classes: Customer
Input: customer : Customer – the target customer to whom the notice is sent. Must not be null.
Output: String – a confirmation message in the format "Notice [noticeId] sent to [customer.name] [customer.surname]".
Pre-condition: customer is not null.
Post-condition: The OverdueNotice's customer field is set to the provided customer. A confirmation string is returned.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
```
```java
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isRented = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isRented = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!isRented) {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    // Sort by dailyPrice ascending
    Collections.sort(availableCars, new Comparator<Car>() {
      public int compare(Car c1, Car c2) {
        return Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice());
      }
    });
    return availableCars;
  }
```

  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {
    Map<Customer, Integer> rentalCounts = new HashMap<Customer, Integer>();
    if (getRentals().isEmpty()) {
      return rentalCounts;
    }
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      Customer c = rental.getCustomer();
      if (rentalCounts.containsKey(c)) {
        rentalCounts.put(c, rentalCounts.get(c) + 1);
      } else {
        rentalCounts.put(c, 1);
      }
    }
    return rentalCounts;
  }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Identifies and returns all cars currently available for rent, sorted by daily price in ascending order.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Create a list to hold available cars.
   *   2. For each car in cars:
   *        - Check if there exists any rental in rentals such that:
   *            rental.car == car AND rental.backDate is null.
   *        - If no such rental exists, add car to available list.
   *   3. Sort the available list by dailyPrice in ascending order.
   *   4. Return the sorted list.
   * Related Features: cars (attribute), rentals (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Car, Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: List<Car> – list of available cars sorted by dailyPrice ascending. Returns empty list if no cars are available or store has no cars.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The returned list contains all cars from the cars list that are not currently rented (i.e., no active rental with null backDate). The list is sorted by dailyPrice in ascending order.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isRented = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isRented = true;
          break;
        }

      if (!isRented) {
        availableCars.add(car);

    Collections.sort(availableCars, new Comparator<Car>() {

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates and returns the total revenue from all rental records in the store.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize total = 0.0.
   *   2. For each rental in the rentals list:
   *        - Add rental.totalPrice to total.
   *   3. Return total.
   * Related Features: rentals (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: double – the sum of totalPrice of all rentals in the store. Returns 0.0 if no rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The returned value equals the sum of totalPrice for all Rental objects in the rentals list.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public double calculateTotalRevenue()
  {
    double total = 0.0;
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      total += rental.getTotalPrice();
    }
    return total;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Finds and returns a list of customers whose rentals are overdue (return date empty and current date past due date).
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
   *   2. Create an empty list of customers (result list).
   *   3. For each rental in rentals:
   *        - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
   *            - Add rental.customer to result list (if not already present, to avoid duplicates).
   *   4. Return result list.
   * Related Features: rentals (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
   * Input: currentDate : EDate – the date to compare against due dates. Can be null.
   * Output: List<Customer> – list of customers with overdue rentals. Returns empty list if currentDate is null or no overdue rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The returned list contains each customer who has at least one rental where backDate is null and currentDate > dueDate. No duplicate customers appear.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    EList<Customer> result = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!result.contains(customer)) {
          result.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return result;
  }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Computes and returns the average daily price of all cars in the store.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If cars list is empty, return 0.0.
   *   2. Initialize sum = 0.0.
   *   3. For each car in cars:
   *        - Add car.dailyPrice to sum.
   *   4. Return sum / cars.size().
   * Related Features: cars (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Car
   * Input: None
   * Output: double – the arithmetic mean of dailyPrice of all cars in the store. Returns 0.0 if no cars exist.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The returned value is either 0.0 (if cars list is empty) or the sum of all car dailyPrices divided by the number of cars.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double determineAverageDailyPrice()
  {
    if (getCars().isEmpty()) {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double sum = 0.0;
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      sum += car.getDailyPrice();
    }
    return sum / getCars().size();
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Counts how many rental records each customer has and returns the result as a mapping.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Create an empty map (EMap) where keys are Customer objects and values are integers.
   *   2. If rentals list is empty, return the empty map.
   *   3. For each rental in rentals:
   *        - Let c = rental.customer
   *        - If c is already a key in the map, increment its count by 1.
   *        - Otherwise, add c with count = 1.
   *   4. Return the map.
   * Related Features: rentals (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
   * Input: None
   * Output: EMap – a map from Customer to Integer, representing the number of rentals per customer. Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The returned map contains exactly one entry per distinct customer found in the rentals list, with the count equal to the number of rentals associated with that customer.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {
    Map<Customer, Integer> rentalCounts = new HashMap<Customer, Integer>();
    if (getRentals().isEmpty()) {
      return rentalCounts;
    }
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      Customer c = rental.getCustomer();
      if (rentalCounts.containsKey(c)) {
        rentalCounts.put(c, rentalCounts.get(c) + 1);
      } else {
        rentalCounts.put(c, 1);
      }
    }
    return rentalCounts;
  }

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Identifies and returns all cars currently available for rent, sorted by daily price in ascending order.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Create a list to hold available cars.
   *   2. For each car in cars:
   *        - Check if there exists any rental in rentals such that:
   *            rental.car == car AND rental.backDate is null.
   *        - If no such rental exists, add car to available list.
   *   3. Sort the available list by dailyPrice in ascending order.
   *   4. Return the sorted list.
   * Related Features: cars (attribute), rentals (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Car, Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: List<Car> – list of available cars sorted by dailyPrice ascending. Returns empty list if no cars are available or store has no cars.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The returned list contains all cars from the cars list that are not currently rented (i.e., no active rental with null backDate). The list is sorted by dailyPrice in ascending order.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isRented = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isRented = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!isRented) {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    Collections.sort(availableCars, new Comparator<Car>() {
      public int compare(Car c1, Car c2) {
        return Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice());
      }
    });
    return availableCars;
  }

   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
   *   2. Create an empty list of customers (result list).
   *   3. For each rental in rentals:
   *        - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
   *            - Add rental.customer to result list (if not already present, to avoid duplicates).
   *   4. Return result list.
   * Related Features: rentals (attribute)
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
   * Input: currentDate : EDate – the date to compare against due dates. Can be null.
   * Output: List<Customer> – list of customers with overdue rentals. Returns empty list if currentDate is null or no overdue rentals exist.

   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    EList<Customer> result = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!result.contains(customer)) {
          result.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return result;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
```

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Calculates the total revenue from all rental records in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0
  2. For each rental in rentals list:
       - If rental.totalPrice is not null:
           total = total + rental.totalPrice
  3. Return total
Related Features: rentals list, Rental.totalPrice
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double - the sum of totalPrice of all rentals. Returns 0.0 if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the computed total revenue; no state changes.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Lists customers whose rentals are overdue (return date empty and current date past due date).
Algorithm:
  1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list
  2. Initialize empty list overdueCustomers
  3. For each rental in rentals list:
       - If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate > rental.dueDate:
           add rental.customer to overdueCustomers (if not already present)
  4. Return overdueCustomers
Related Features: rentals list, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input: currentDate : EDate - the date to compare against due dates; may be null.
Output: List<Customer> - list of customers with overdue rentals. Returns empty list if none or if currentDate is null.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the list of overdue customers; no state changes.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identifies cars that are currently available (not tied to an active rental with empty return date) and returns them sorted by daily price ascending.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize empty list availableCars
  2. For each car in cars list:
       - Check if there is any rental in rentals list where:
           rental.car == car AND rental.backDate is null
       - If no such rental exists, add car to availableCars
  3. Sort availableCars by dailyPrice in ascending order
  4. Return availableCars
Related Features: cars list, rentals list, Rental.car, Rental.backDate, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> - list of available cars sorted by daily price ascending. Returns empty list if none available.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the sorted list of available cars; no state changes.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Computes the average daily price of all cars in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. If cars list is empty, return 0.0
  2. Initialize sum = 0.0
  3. For each car in cars list:
       sum = sum + car.dailyPrice
  4. average = sum / size of cars list
  5. Return average
Related Features: cars list, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double - the arithmetic mean of dailyPrice of all cars. Returns 0.0 if no cars exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the computed average; no state changes.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts how many rental records belong to each customer and returns the result as a customer-to-count mapping.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty map (EMap) customerCount
  2. For each rental in rentals list:
       - Get rental.customer
       - If customer already exists in customerCount:
           increment its count by 1
       - Else:
           add customer to map with count = 1
  3. Return customerCount
Related Features: rentals list, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap - a mapping from Customer objects to Integer counts. Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the computed mapping; no state changes.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Sends an overdue notice to the specified customer by generating a notice message.
Algorithm:

Related Classes: Customer
Input: customer : Customer - the target customer to receive the notice; must not be null.
Output: String - the formatted notice message (e.g., "Notice [noticeId] sent to [name] [surname] at [address]").
Pre-condition: customer is not null and has valid name, surname, and address.
Post-condition: A notice message string is returned; no persistent state is changed.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

```java
   * Summary: Identifies cars that are currently available (not tied to an active rental with empty return date) and returns them sorted by daily price ascending.
```

   * @generated NOT

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isRented = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isRented = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!isRented) {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    // Sort availableCars by dailyPrice in ascending order
    availableCars.sort((c1, c2) -> Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice()));
    return availableCars;
  }

      total = total + rental.getTotalPrice();
    }
    return total;

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!overdueCustomers.contains(customer)) {
          overdueCustomers.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return overdueCustomers;
  }

    if (getCars().isEmpty()) {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double sum = 0.0;
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      sum = sum + car.getDailyPrice();
    }
    double average = sum / getCars().size();
    return average;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Counts how many rental records belong to each customer and returns the result as a customer-to-count mapping.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize an empty map (EMap) customerCount
   *   2. For each rental in rentals list:
   *        - Get rental.customer
   *        - If customer already exists in customerCount:
   *            increment its count by 1
   *        - Else:
   *            add customer to map with count = 1
   *   3. Return customerCount
   * Related Features: rentals list, Rental.customer
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
   * Input: None
   * Output: EMap - a mapping from Customer objects to Integer counts. Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: Returns the computed mapping; no state changes.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {
    Map<Customer, Integer> customerCount = new HashMap<Customer, Integer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
      if (customerCount.containsKey(customer)) {
        customerCount.put(customer, customerCount.get(customer) + 1);
      } else {
        customerCount.put(customer, 1);
      }
    }
    return customerCount;
  }

   * Summary: Identifies cars that are currently available (not tied to an active rental with empty return date) and returns them sorted by daily price ascending.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize empty list availableCars
   *   2. For each car in cars list:
   *        - Check if there is any rental in rentals list where:
   *            rental.car == car AND rental.backDate is null
   *        - If no such rental exists, add car to availableCars
   *   3. Sort availableCars by dailyPrice in ascending order
   *   4. Return availableCars
   * Related Features: cars list, rentals list, Rental.car, Rental.backDate, Car.dailyPrice
   * Related Classes: Car, Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: List<Car> - list of available cars sorted by daily price ascending. Returns empty list if none available.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: Returns the sorted list of available cars; no state changes.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isRented = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isRented = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!isRented) {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    availableCars.sort((c1, c2) -> Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice()));
    return availableCars;
  }

   *   1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list
   *   2. Initialize empty list overdueCustomers

   *   4. Return overdueCustomers
   * Related Features: rentals list, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
   * Input: currentDate : EDate - the date to compare against due dates; may be null.
   * Output: List<Customer> - list of customers with overdue rentals. Returns empty list if none or if currentDate is null.
   * Pre-condition: None

   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!overdueCustomers.contains(customer)) {
          overdueCustomers.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return overdueCustomers;
  }
